# Audio Check

These screens allow you to troubleshoot the audio function. Select the Speaker Check icon.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Speaker Check

- Make sure the audio system is off before this diagnosis.

- While doing the diagnosis, the fader and the balance positions are set to the center.

This diagnosis checks the audio speakers individually.

Select the UP icon or the Down icon to check speakers. A tone should sound from the speaker.

When you select the Up icon, the system checks the speakers in this order: -> -> -> -> -> -> -> -> -> -> ->

When you select the Down icon, the system checks the speakers in this order: -> -> -> -> -> -> -> -> -> -> ->

Display Message | Vehicle Test Condition

FR-L | You should hear a tone from the driver's door speaker.

FRTW-L | You should hear a tone from the left front tweeter.

*1 | CNT1 | You should hear a tone from the front center speaker.

FR-R | You should hear a tone from the front passenger's door speaker.

FRTW-R | You should hear a tone from the right front tweeter.

RR2-R *1/RR1-R *2 | You should hear a tone from the right rear (door *3) speaker.

RR2TW-R *1/RR1TW-R *2 | You should hear a tone from the right rear (door *3) tweeter.

*1 | SW1 | You should hear a tone from the subwoofer.

RR2-L *1/RR1-L *2 | You should hear a tone from the left rear (door *3) speaker.

RR2TW-L *1/RR1TW-L *2 | You should hear a tone from the left rear (door *3) tweeter.

ALL | You should hear a tone from all speakers.

*1: With stereo amplifier

*2: Without stereo amplifier

*3: 5-door

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

This screen initializes the back-up data in the audio-navigation unit. Select the All Clear icon.

All Clear

## Chunk 3107: Save User's Memory

- Title: Save User's Memory
- Source path: `pages\2181.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_1f0ce4178ead`
- Images: `images\GHH410745.jpeg`, `images\GHH410746.jpeg`, `images\GHH410747.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\13332.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Save User's Memory

When replacing the audio-navigation unit, this function allows the technician to transfer the customer's personal data to the new audio-navigation unit. The transferred information includes their setup settings and personal addresses. The dealer inserts a USB device into USB port A or B, and then selects the Save Users Memory function. The two functions in this diagnostic screen are Export and Import. Export saves the customer's data to the USB device, and Import moves the USB device files to the new audio-navigation unit.

This function does not start until you connect a USB device to the USB port.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

1. Select the Export icon to move the customer's data from the original audio-navigation unit to the USB device. Select the Yes icon on the Export User Data Confirmation screen. The process takes only a couple of seconds. The system stores.DAT files on the USB device.

NOTE: If the Export icon is grayed out, check the USB port.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

2. After replacing the new audio-navigation unit, allow the system to boot up. Insert the USB device into USB port A or B and enter the Save Users Memory in the System Diagnostic Mode.

3. Select the Import icon to move the two files stored by the Export process from the USB device to the new audio-navigation unit. Select the Yes icon on the Import User Data Confirmation screen. When the transfer is finished (a few seconds) the system automatically reboots. After the system reboots, remove the USB device from the USB port.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 3108: Navigation System Diagnostic Mode: LaneWatch

- Title: Navigation System Diagnostic Mode: LaneWatch
- Source path: `pages\2182.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_071a3f9e5fe9`
- Images: `images\GHH410748.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\13333.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Navigation System Diagnostic Mode: LaneWatch

This diagnosis checks the LaneWatch status.

- If Connection shows hyphen (-), check for hard error codes .

- Error Status: Indicates LaneWatch camera status OK or NG. If Error Status shows NG, replace the LaneWatch camera . When the LaneWatch camera is disconnected, the status shows hyphen (-).

- If Error Status shows OK, press the HOME button to display the LaneWatch camera image. To return to the previous screen, press the HOME button again.

- LaneWatch Switch: LaneWatch switch status on or off. When the LaneWatch switch is pressing, the status shows ON. When the LaneWatch switch is released, the status shows hyphen (-).

- B-CAN: Checks whether the audio-navigation unit has received specific B-CAN data from the body control module. If B-CAN shows NG, check for B-CAN DTCs with the HDS. If the DTCs are not detected, check for B-CAN lines continuity between the audio-navigation unit and the body control module.

- If Connection, Error Status, LaneWatch Switch, and B-CAN shows OK, the LaneWatch camera aiming procedure is available. If the LaneWatch camera need to aiming, refer to the How to Start LaneWatch Camera Aiming . When the aiming fails, Aiming shows NG.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

NOTE: If the automatic lighting/rain sensor connector is connected after the 12 volt battery terminal is connected, the automatic lighting/rain sensor is not confirmed by the body control module. If the body control module does not confirm the automatic lighting/rain sensor, the automatic wiper system does not work. Install the automatic lighting/rain sensor (B) onto the mounting bracket (C), then push in on the lock (D).

Connect the automatic lighting/rain sensor connector first, then connect the 12 volt battery terminal. 1. Connect the connector (A). 2. Install the automatic lighting/rain sensor (B) onto the mounting bracket (C), then push in on the lock (D).

- If the automatic lighting/rain sensor connector is connected after the 12 volt battery terminal is connected, the automatic lighting/rain sensor is not confirmed by the body control module.

- If the body control module does not confirm the automatic lighting/rain sensor, the automatic wiper system does not work.

- Connect the automatic lighting/rain sensor connector first, then connect the 12 volt battery terminal.

1. Connect the connector (A).

2. Install the automatic lighting/rain sensor (B) onto the mounting bracket (C), then push in on the lock (D).

- Camera Cover - Install
````

# Exterior Seal Removal and Installation: Installation

Front Bumper Seal

- Front Bumper Seal - Install 1. From under the front bumper (A), place a mark on the middle of the front bumper. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Clean the front bumper bonding surface with a sponge dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the surface. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Install the front bumper seal (A) to the front bumper (B) from the mark placed in step 1 as shown. NOTE: Do not use an adhesive primer. Do not run off the edges (C). If necessary, cut the excess front bumper seal. After attaching the front bumper seal, press the front bumper seal firmly.

1. From under the front bumper (A), place a mark on the middle of the front bumper.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

2. Clean the front bumper bonding surface with a sponge dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the surface.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

3. Install the front bumper seal (A) to the front bumper (B) from the mark placed in step 1 as shown.

NOTE:

- Do not use an adhesive primer.

- Do not run off the edges (C). If necessary, cut the excess front bumper seal.

- After attaching the front bumper seal, press the front bumper seal firmly.

Side Sill Panel Seal

- Side Sill Panel Seal - Install 1. Clean the side sill panel bonding surface with a sponge dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the surface. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Install the side sill panel seal (A) to the side sill panel (B) from the edge as shown. NOTE: Do not use an adhesive primer. Do not run off the edge (C). If necessary, cut the excess side sill panel seal. After attaching the front bumper seal, press the front bumper seal firmly.

1. Clean the side sill panel bonding surface with a sponge dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the surface.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

2. Install the side sill panel seal (A) to the side sill panel (B) from the edge as shown.

NOTE:

- Do not use an adhesive primer.

- Do not run off the edge (C). If necessary, cut the excess side sill panel seal.

- After attaching the front bumper seal, press the front bumper seal firmly.

Rear Bumper Seal

- Rear Bumper Seal - Install 1. From under the rear bumper (A), place a mark on the middle of the rear bumper. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Clean the rear bumper bonding surface with a sponge dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the surface. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Install the rear bumper seal (A) to the rear bumper (B) from the mark placed in step 1 as shown. NOTE: Do not use an adhesive primer. Do not run off the edges (C). If necessary, cut the excess rear bumper seal. After attaching the front bumper seal, press the front bumper seal firmly.

1. From under the rear bumper (A), place a mark on the middle of the rear bumper.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

2. Clean the rear bumper bonding surface with a sponge dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the surface.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

3. Install the rear bumper seal (A) to the rear bumper (B) from the mark placed in step 1 as shown.

NOTE:

- Do not use an adhesive primer.

- Do not run off the edges (C). If necessary, cut the excess rear bumper seal.

- After attaching the front bumper seal, press the front bumper seal firmly.

Pull out the front bumper (A) at the wheel arch area to release it from the hooks (B) on the side spacer. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 4. With the help of an assistant, while pulling the front bumper (A) away from the side spacer, release the bumper from the hooks (B) on the upper beam. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 5. With fog light: Disconnect the connectors (A). 6. Remove the front bumper (B).

# Windshield Wiper Control Unit/Motor Removal and Installation: Installation

- Windshield Wiper Motor - Install Fig 1: Exploded View Of Windshield Wiper Motor Components With Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. NOTE: Bolt threads are not provided on the new wiper motor. Install the wiper motor to the linkage as follows: 1. Before installing the wiper motor, connect the wiper motor connector, and turn the wiper/washer switch ON to (LO) or (HI) position, then OFF to return the wiper motor shaft to the park position. NOTE: Do not use the windshield wiper/washer switch (AUTO) or (INT) position in this step. Apply multipurpose grease to the moving parts. Check the wiper motor operation. 2. Set the motor (A) and the wiper linkage assembly (B), and tighten the bolts until the bolt head just contacts the linkage. NOTE: Install the provided self-tapping bolts. Keep the bolts perfectly straight while bolting them in. Do not use air tools and impact wrenches. 3. Loosen the bolts, then retighten the bolts to the specified torque. 4. Align the marks (C) on the link (D) and the wiper linkage assembly, and install the link. 5. Install the connection rod (E).

NOTE: Bolt threads are not provided on the new wiper motor. Install the wiper motor to the linkage as follows: 1. Before installing the wiper motor, connect the wiper motor connector, and turn the wiper/washer switch ON to (LO) or (HI) position, then OFF to return the wiper motor shaft to the park position. NOTE: Do not use the windshield wiper/washer switch (AUTO) or (INT) position in this step. Apply multipurpose grease to the moving parts. Check the wiper motor operation. 2. Set the motor (A) and the wiper linkage assembly (B), and tighten the bolts until the bolt head just contacts the linkage. NOTE: Install the provided self-tapping bolts. Keep the bolts perfectly straight while bolting them in. Do not use air tools and impact wrenches. 3. Loosen the bolts, then retighten the bolts to the specified torque. 4. Align the marks (C) on the link (D) and the wiper linkage assembly, and install the link. 5. Install the connection rod (E).

1. Before installing the wiper motor, connect the wiper motor connector, and turn the wiper/washer switch ON to (LO) or (HI) position, then OFF to return the wiper motor shaft to the park position.

NOTE:

- Do not use the windshield wiper/washer switch (AUTO) or (INT) position in this step.

- Apply multipurpose grease to the moving parts.

- Check the wiper motor operation.

2. Set the motor (A) and the wiper linkage assembly (B), and tighten the bolts until the bolt head just contacts the linkage.

NOTE:

3. Loosen the bolts, then retighten the bolts to the specified torque.

# Removal and Installation

NOTE:

- Before removal and installation, turn the wiper/washer switch ON to (LO) or (HI) position, then OFF to return the motor shaft to the park position.

- Do not use the wiper/washer switch (AUTO) or (INT) position in this step.

Windshield

- Windshield Wiper Arm Assembly - Remove Fig 1: Windshield Wiper Arm Assembly Components With Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Remove the caps (A). 2. Remove the windshield wiper arms (B).

1. Remove the caps (A). 2. Remove the windshield wiper arms (B).

2. Remove the windshield wiper arms (B).

- Windshield Wiper Blade Assembly - Remove

- All Removed Parts - Install 1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal.

1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal.

- Windshield Wiper Arm/Nozzle - Adjust

Rear Window (With Rear Window Wiper)

- Rear Window Wiper Arm Assembly - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Remove the cap (A). 2. Remove the rear window wiper arm (B).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Remove the cap (A). 2. Remove the rear window wiper arm (B).

2. Remove the rear window wiper arm (B).

- Rear Window Wiper Blade Assembly - Remove

- All Removed Parts - Install 1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal.

1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal.

- Rear Window Wiper Arm/Nozzle - Adjust
````

# Wiper Blade Removal and Installation: Removal

Windshield

- Locking Back Wiper Arms - Procedure 1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. 2. Turn and hold the wiper/washer switch to the MIST position, and then turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. 3. Release the wiper/washer switch. NOTE: The wiper arm stops at the lock back position. 4. Lift the wiper arm(s) off the windshield.

Find the end of the blade holder labeled "SET" (A), and then pull back the wiper blade (B). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 5. Pull out the wiper blade (A) through the wiper blade opening. - Windshield Washer Motor - Test Except Japan production Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Japan production Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Test the washer motor (A) by connecting 12 volt battery power to terminal No. 1 and ground to terminal No. 2 of the washer motor. The motor should run. If the washer motor does not run or fails to run smoothly, replace the windshield washer motor. If the washer motor runs smoothly, but little or no washer fluid is pumped, check for a disconnected or blocked washer hose, or a clogged washer motor outlet.

# Center Silencer Type (Type-R)

Numbered call-outs in figure pertain to list numbers in following procedure.

*1 | 2020-21

- Rear Bumper Center Trim - Remove

- Rear Bumper Side Trim - Remove

- Rear Bumper Lower Trim - Remove

- Rear Bumper Face - Remove

- Rear Towing Hook Cover - Remove

- Finisher Cover - Remove

- Rear Bumper Side Fin - Remove

- Reflector - Remove

- Rear Bumper Side Molding - Remove (2020-21)

- Rear Bumper Side Mesh - Remove (2020-21)

- All Disassembled Parts - Assemble 1. Assemble the parts in the reverse order of disassembly.

1. Assemble the parts in the reverse order of disassembly.
````

## Chunk 3229: Hood Adjustment: Adjustment

- Title: Hood Adjustment: Adjustment
- Source path: `pages\2317.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_2c744c70c205`
- Images: `images\GHH407495.jpeg`, `images\GHH407496.jpeg`, `images\GHH407497.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3000.html`, `pages\25504.html`, `pages\14256.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Hood Adjustment: Adjustment

- Front Grille Cover - Remove

- Hood - Adjust 1. Slightly loosen the hood hinge mounting bolts (A, B) and the hood latch mounting bolts (C). Fig 1: Hood Adjustment Components With Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Adjust the hood (D) alignment in the following sequence: Adjust the hood right and left, as well as forward and rearward, by using the elongated holes in the hood hinges (E). Turn the hood edge cushions (F), in or out as necessary, to make the hood fit flush with the body at the front and side edges. 3. Adjust the hood latch (G) to obtain the proper height at the forward edge, and move the latch right or left until the striker (H) is centered in the latch. 4. Tighten each bolt to the specified torque. 5. Check that the hood opens properly and closes securely. 6. Apply the touch-up paint to the hinge mounting bolts and around the hinges, and let the paint dry. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 7. Apply the multipurpose grease to the hood latch and the hood hinges as indicated by the arrows.

1. Slightly loosen the hood hinge mounting bolts (A, B) and the hood latch mounting bolts (C).

2. Adjust the hood (D) alignment in the following sequence:

- Adjust the hood right and left, as well as forward and rearward, by using the elongated holes in the hood hinges (E).

- Turn the hood edge cushions (F), in or out as necessary, to make the hood fit flush with the body at the front and side edges.

3. Adjust the hood latch (G) to obtain the proper height at the forward edge, and move the latch right or left until the striker (H) is centered in the latch.

4. Tighten each bolt to the specified torque.

5. Check that the hood opens properly and closes securely.

6. Apply the touch-up paint to the hinge mounting bolts and around the hinges, and let the paint dry.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 7. Apply the multipurpose grease to the hood latch and the hood hinges as indicated by the arrows.

- Front Grille Cover - Install
````

## Chunk 3230: Tailgate Adjustment (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021): Adjustment

- Title: Tailgate Adjustment (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021): Adjustment
- Source path: `pages\2318.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_10c22f881649`
- Images: `images\GHH407498.jpeg`, `images\GHH407499.jpeg`, `images\GHH407500.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3001.html`, `pages\25505.html`, `pages\14257.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Tailgate Adjustment (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021): Adjustment

NOTE: How to read the torque specifications .

- Tailgate - Adjust Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Remove the striker cap (A) from the rear trim panel (B). 2. Remove the tailgate support strut . Fig 1: Tailgate Adjustment Components With Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Slightly loosen the tailgate hinge bolts (A) and the striker bolts (B). 4. Adjust the tailgate (C) alignment in the following sequence: Adjust the tailgate hinges (D) right and left, as well as forward and rearward, by using the elongated holes. Take care not to hit the rear window when loosening the bolts. Turn the tailgate edge cushions (E), in or out as necessary, to make the tailgate fit flush with the body at the rear and side edges. Adjust the fit between the tailgate and the tailgate opening by moving the striker (F), and adjust the striker right or left until it is centered in the tailgate latch (G). 5. Tighten each bolt to the specified torque. 6. Make sure the tailgate opens properly and locks securely. 7. Install the tailgate support strut . 8. Apply touch-up paint to the hinge mounting bolts and around the hinges, and let the paint dry. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 9. Apply the multipurpose grease to the pivot area of the tailgate hinges (A) as indicated by the arrows.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Remove the striker cap (A) from the rear trim panel (B).

2. Remove the tailgate support strut .

3. Slightly loosen the tailgate hinge bolts (A) and the striker bolts (B).

4. Adjust the tailgate (C) alignment in the following sequence:

- Adjust the tailgate hinges (D) right and left, as well as forward and rearward, by using the elongated holes. Take care not to hit the rear window when loosening the bolts.

- Turn the tailgate edge cushions (E), in or out as necessary, to make the tailgate fit flush with the body at the rear and side edges.

- Adjust the fit between the tailgate and the tailgate opening by moving the striker (F), and adjust the striker right or left until it is centered in the tailgate latch (G).

5. Tighten each bolt to the specified torque.

6. Make sure the tailgate opens properly and locks securely.

7. Install the tailgate support strut .

8. Apply touch-up paint to the hinge mounting bolts and around the hinges, and let the paint dry.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 9. Apply the multipurpose grease to the pivot area of the tailgate hinges (A) as indicated by the arrows.

- Water Leaks - Check
````

````text
# Trunk Lid Adjustment: Adjustment

- Rear Shelf - Remove

- Rear Panel Cap - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Trunk Lid - Adjust 1. Slightly loosen the trunk lid hinge bolts (A) and the striker bolts (B). Fig 1: Trunk Lid Adjustment Components With Torque Specifications Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Adjust the trunk lid (C) alignment in the following sequence: Adjust the trunk lid hinges (D) right and left, as well as forward and rearward, by using the elongated holes. Take care not to hit the rear window when loosening the bolts. Turn the trunk lid edge cushions (E), in or out as necessary, to make the trunk lid fit flush with the body at the rear and side edges. Adjust the fit between the trunk lid and the trunk lid opening by moving the striker (F). 3. Tighten each bolt to the specified torque. 4. Make sure the trunk lid opens properly and locks securely.

1. Slightly loosen the trunk lid hinge bolts (A) and the striker bolts (B).

2. Adjust the trunk lid (C) alignment in the following sequence:

- Adjust the trunk lid hinges (D) right and left, as well as forward and rearward, by using the elongated holes. Take care not to hit the rear window when loosening the bolts.

- Turn the trunk lid edge cushions (E), in or out as necessary, to make the trunk lid fit flush with the body at the rear and side edges.

- Adjust the fit between the trunk lid and the trunk lid opening by moving the striker (F).

3. Tighten each bolt to the specified torque.

4. Make sure the trunk lid opens properly and locks securely.

- Rear Shelf - Install
````

## Chunk 3232: Wiper Arm/Nozzle Adjustment (2/4-door): Adjustment

- Title: Wiper Arm/Nozzle Adjustment (2/4-door): Adjustment
- Source path: `pages\2320.html`

- Images: `images\GHH407503.jpeg`, `images\GHH407504.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3003.html`, `pages\25507.html`, `pages\14259.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Wiper Arm/Nozzle Adjustment (2/4-door): Adjustment

Except Japan Production

- Wiper Arm/Nozzle - Adjustment NOTICE: Adjust the washer nozzles by gently gripping, and then moving, the outside of each nozzle (A). Do not insert tools into the nozzle hole (B), as it may cause the washer fluid to spray incorrectly. Wiper Arms Auto-Stop Position 1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then turn the wiper switch ON, and then back OFF. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. When the wiper arms stop at the auto-stop position, make sure that they are at the correct position. a: Position at about 54.8 mm (2.157 in) from the end of the windshield to the end cap of the wiper blade (C). b: Position at about 51.6 mm (2.031 in) from the cowl cover (D) to the end cap of the wiper blade. Washer Nozzle Position 3. When you turn on the washer, make sure 50% or more of the washer fluid lands within the spray area. If the spray area is not within the standard positions, adjust the nozzle(s). d: Position at about 251 mm (9.88 in) from the windshield center line (E). e: Position at about 159 mm (6.26 in) from the end of black ceramic gradation area (F) at the lower windshield. f: 2.6 degrees angle to the vertical line (G).

NOTICE:

- Adjust the washer nozzles by gently gripping, and then moving, the outside of each nozzle (A).

- Do not insert tools into the nozzle hole (B), as it may cause the washer fluid to spray incorrectly.

Wiper Arms Auto-Stop Position

1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then turn the wiper switch ON, and then back OFF.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

2. When the wiper arms stop at the auto-stop position, make sure that they are at the correct position.

a: Position at about 54.8 mm (2.157 in) from the end of the windshield to the end cap of the wiper blade (C).

b: Position at about 51.6 mm (2.031 in) from the cowl cover (D) to the end cap of the wiper blade.

Washer Nozzle Position

3. When you turn on the washer, make sure 50% or more of the washer fluid lands within the spray area. If the spray area is not within the standard positions, adjust the nozzle(s).

d: Position at about 251 mm (9.88 in) from the windshield center line (E).

e: Position at about 159 mm (6.26 in) from the end of black ceramic gradation area (F) at the lower windshield.

f: 2.6 degrees angle to the vertical line (G).

Japan Production

- Wiper Arm/Nozzle - Adjustment NOTICE: Adjust the washer nozzles by gently gripping, and then moving, the outside of each nozzle (A). Do not insert tools into the nozzle hole (B), as it may cause the washer fluid to spray incorrectly. Wiper Arms Auto-Stop Position 1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then turn the wiper switch ON, and then back OFF. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. When the wiper arms stop at the auto-stop position, make sure that they are at the correct position. a: Position at about 40.3 mm (1.587 in) from the end of the windshield to the wiper blade (C). b: Position at about 38.5 mm (1.516 in) from the cowl cover (D) to the end cap of the wiper blade. Washer Nozzle Position 3. When you turn on the washer, make sure 50 % or more of the washer fluid lands within the spray area. If the spray area is not within the standard positions, adjust the nozzle(s). d: Position at about 250.3 mm (9.854 in) from the windshield center line (E). e: Position at about 142.6 mm (5.614 in) from the end of black ceramic gradation area (F) at the lower windshield.

NOTICE:

- Adjust the washer nozzles by gently gripping, and then moving, the outside of each nozzle (A).

- Do not insert tools into the nozzle hole (B), as it may cause the washer fluid to spray incorrectly.

Wiper Arms Auto-Stop Position

1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then turn the wiper switch ON, and then back OFF.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

2. When the wiper arms stop at the auto-stop position, make sure that they are at the correct position.

a: Position at about 40.3 mm (1.587 in) from the end of the windshield to the wiper blade (C).

b: Position at about 38.5 mm (1.516 in) from the cowl cover (D) to the end cap of the wiper blade.

Washer Nozzle Position

3. When you turn on the washer, make sure 50 % or more of the washer fluid lands within the spray area. If the spray area is not within the standard positions, adjust the nozzle(s).

d: Position at about 250.3 mm (9.854 in) from the windshield center line (E).

e: Position at about 142.6 mm (5.614 in) from the end of black ceramic gradation area (F) at the lower windshield.
````
